Item 3 (the provider token and the `twilio_auth_token` variable "linked") was a misreading of masked secrets: `GET /webhooks/variables` answers `••••oken`, which equals the mask of the provider's `twilio-test-token`, so the PATCH kept the stored value; the two tokens are separate and undocumented. Item 4 was partly done already (`/v1/Services` works; its PhoneNumbers and `IncomingPhoneNumbers.json` do not). Item 9 is a bug (#316): the code-first OTP pattern allows nothing between `is your` and `code`. Owner decisions: A23 for 1.4.0 (SM-19 #310, SM-20 #311, SM-21 #312, EM-23 #309, PL-20 #313, PL-21 #314; the OTP fix ships with it as EM-05.6, added and ticked in its fixing PR, since a new open criterion on the accepted EM-05 would fail the acceptance check); A24, the interactive launcher (PL-22 #315: a bare `sendmock` in a terminal asks for services and ports, remembers them in `last-run.toml`, Enter twice to run again; `--last`, `--services` and `--no-input` never ask; the Docker image sets `SENDMOCK_NO_INPUT=1`); PL-21 widened from static responses to HTTP Catcher bins on a provider's own base URL in every area, with signed follow-up webhooks and an agent flow (skill section, MCP `fill-provider-gap` prompt); A25, the storage mock for 2.0.0 (new area ST, `acceptance/10-storage.md`, #317 to #322; label `area: storage` created). 4. **Bug #238: retention pruning is invisible to the UI. Fixed and merged (PR #240, `154e19a9`, 2026-11-13; #238 closed):** `prune_traffic` publishes `traffic.pruned` (`{ "pruned_count": n }`) when any row went, rules cannot pick it (like `webhook.delivered`), and the UI refreshes every live list and the stats on it; new criterion PL-08.5, proven by `traffic_retention_api.rs`, Vitest and the live `retention-prune.spec.ts` (`max_emails: 3`, five emails, the list, count and Mail badge drop to 3 without a reload). Once CI passes and #240 merges, close #238. The bug as found: The retention task (`crates/sendmock/src/retention_task.rs`, every 10 s, default newest 1,000 emails) publishes no event, and since W19 (#224) new emails are written straight into the cached lists, so past 1,000 emails the count and list drift above what is stored until a refresh (a pruned row opens as a 404). It made `web/tests/live/realtime-burst.spec.ts` fail (a sweep during the 5,000-email burst; 3 of 4 runs on 2026-11-13, on #232 and #235); since #235 the burst test turns retention off (`PATCH /settings` with `max_emails: 0`) and restores it after. Fix: publish an event when traffic is pruned and refresh the affected lists and stats on it; a live test proves a prune refreshes the list.

Design in [30](30-phase-5.md#design-notes) (W20 to W23).
6. **W25, test builds of every pull request (DS-11, #245), planned 2026-11-14 (owner request), for the next beta. Done and merged into `develop` (#246, merge commit `c9cddfe8`, 2026-11-14; DS-11 accepted; #245 closed by the roadmap sync):** every pull request (into any branch) and every push to `develop` and `main` runs a Test builds workflow that calls the release build with publishing off, tests each of the six archives on its own platform and uploads it as `sendmock-<target triple>` (14 days for pull requests, 30 for `develop` and `main`), linked from the run summary and one updated comment on the pull request (forks get the summary only). Design in [09](09-distribution.md#test-builds-of-every-pull-request-planned-2026-11-14-owner-request-ds-11) and [30](30-phase-5.md#design-notes). Built on the plan's pull request #246 (owner request): `build-archives.yml` (the UI and six-target build, moved out of `release.yml`, which calls it), `test-builds.yml`, `scripts/test-builds-report.ts`, the install page's steps. The first Test builds run passed (43569610917, 2026-11-14): all six targets built, tested on their own platform and attached, with the run summary and one pull request comment linking them; DS-11 is accepted. #246 was retitled `feat:[#245] test builds of every pull request`, went green after the test fix and is merged; it ships with beta.4.
7. **Before 1.0.0 (2026-11-14), merged as #257 (`f39a4445`; #255 and #256 closed):** none of the 14 workspace crates is on crates.io, and crates.io sets a trusted publisher only on a crate that exists, so the OIDC-only crates.io job would fail at 1.0.0 (#255). Owner decision: a one-time token, as with npm. The owner saved `CARGO_REGISTRY_TOKEN` (`publish-new` and `publish-update`, crates `sendmock*`); the crates.io job uses it while it is set and OIDC otherwise, and `scripts/release/crates-publish.ts` publishes the crates in dependency order, skipping versions crates.io has and waiting out its rate limit. On the same branch (owner decision: one combined branch), the README's video link becomes a card with a play button and the length (#256). After 1.0.0: set `registry-publish.yml` as the trusted publisher on all 14 crates and delete the secret (runbook step 7).
8. **Runbook step 7 in two releases (owner decision, 2026-11-14):** `bump=major` from `0.1.0-beta.4` makes `1.0.0-beta.1`, not `1.0.0` (every stable goes through a beta; `scripts/version/next-version.ts`), and `bump=stable` would finish `0.1.0`. So: dry run and real run of `bump=major`, merge the `1.0.0-beta.1` release PR, Install channels on it; then dry run and real run of `bump=stable` for `1.0.0` (no `feat:` or `fix:` may land in between), the promote PR into `main`, Install channels on `1.0.0`, the crates.io trusted publishers and deleting `CARGO_REGISTRY_TOKEN`. The runbook and [30](30-phase-5.md) owner step 7 now say so.
9. **Then runbook steps 8 to 10:** the Nixpkgs pull request and every channel, `main` as the default branch (right after `1.1.0` is released, owner decision 2026-11-16), the GitHub MCP Registry.

Step 4, encrypted secrets with a passphrase (design in [32](32-after-1-0.md#a18-share-and-move-a-setup-versioned-export-and-import-pl-18), "Secrets in practice"): an encrypted secret is the string `encrypted:v1:<salt>:<sealed>` in place of the `${ENV_VAR}` reference (not `{ encrypted = "..." }` as first planned: provider and gateway credentials sit inside definition JSON text, and the schema keeps secrets as strings). New in `crates/sendmock-ops/src/setup/`: `setup_secret_cipher.rs` (`EncryptedSecret`, `SetupSecretKey`: AES-256-GCM, PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 600,000 iterations, 16-byte salt, nonce per secret, passphrase of 8 characters or more), `setup_secret_opener.rs` (`SetupSecretOpener`, `blank_encrypted_secrets`), `setup_secret_writer.rs` (`SetupSecretWriter` Strategy: `SecretReferenceWriter`, which took over `to_secret_reference`, and `EncryptedSecretWriter`) and `setup_secret_error.rs` (`setup_passphrase_required`, `setup_passphrase_wrong`). `SeedSectionAdapter::export_items` takes the writer; the macro's `writes_secrets` marks the four adapters that hold secrets (endpoints, variables, SMS and email providers, payment gateways). `write_setup_text` takes a `&dyn SetupSecretWriter`; `apply_setup_text` takes `SetupImportOptions` (`source_path`, `passphrase`) and opens the secrets after resolving `${ENV_VAR}` references, before anything is applied; the seed state keeps the file with encrypted secrets emptied, so reset needs no passphrase. The seed validator accepts an encrypted secret on secret keys and names a damaged one. `ring` is now a dependency of `sendmock-ops` (already in the binary through rustls). Tests (not run locally; fmt, clippy and `cargo check` are clean): unit tests in each new file and in `seed_string_rules.rs`, and `crates/sendmock-api/tests/setup_encrypted_secrets_api.rs` (TOML and JSON exports move all three secret kinds to a fresh instance; no passphrase or a wrong one applies nothing; the seed state holds no secret and reset keeps them). PL-18.6 is not ticked yet: its surfaces come in step 6. Its first CI run failed six ways, fixed in `fix:[#236] name simulate items by their action and keep the checks green`: the spec missed the `chosen` trigger (`bun run openapi` in `web/`); clippy found `InboundSmsOperation::execute` at 51 lines (the media check moved to `accept_inbound_media`); `webhook_targets_api.rs` read the rules list as `{ items }` (it is a bare array); a Simulate item's accessible name included its "No webhook" or "Webhooks: ..." hint, and a Choose item's name contains its action, so exact and substring lookups of `Refund...`, `Capture`, `Expire checkout`, `Simulate read`, `Hard bounce`, `Insufficient funds` and the Send a sample buttons broke (the item is now named by its label through `aria-labelledby` with the hint as `aria-describedby`, the inventory and entries drop the hint, and those lookups are exact); UI-05.1 tabbed 25 times into the gateway sheet and landed on an icon button whose tooltip took the first Escape (the test now closes an open tooltip first); and the embedded UI is 2,107,884 bytes, 10.7 KB over the 2 MB budget (UI-06.3; owner choice: measure in CI, then trim, so the budget test now prints the 15 largest dist files). The fix commit `de93e348` passed every e2e shard, the live UI suite and parity; it failed only the CLI snapshot (now copied by hand from insta's diff: `--webhook` on 11 commands and `sendmock webhook rule match`) and the budget (2,107,976 bytes; largest files: the app script 476 KB, the code editor 349 KB, the CSS 126 KB, Settings 101 KB, nothing obviously wasted). Owner choice: a per-module report, then trim. The next commit adds `reportLargestModules` in `vite.config.ts` (only when the budget test sets `SENDMOCK_MODULE_REPORT`, written outside `dist`), so the budget test prints the 30 largest source modules, and keeps Tailwind from scanning `tests/`, the mock fixtures and the generated API document (`@source not` in `src/index.css`). That commit (`eb31cd21`) passed everything but the budget (2,107,668 bytes; the Tailwind exclusions saved about 300 bytes). The module report showed the build is libraries (React DOM 517 KB, CodeMirror, sonner, the virtual list, Radix, the router; `cn` is as large as `clsx` plus `tailwind-merge`) with no app module over 14 KB; the only clear cut, the Radix slider (about 10 KB), would barely cover the overage. Owner decision: raise UI-06.3 to 2.25 MB, recorded in the criterion, `07-ui.md`, `09-distribution.md` and `18-phase-u3.md`. That commit (`900ccd4f`) passed 72 checks; the budget step passed and the Web UI job then failed its next step, the OpenAPI contract, which the earlier runs never reached: `WebhookRuleMatchRequest.subject_id` had no `@example` (added, spec and references regenerated with `bun run openapi`). **W22 is done:** `f77778f0` passed all 73 checks and the roadmap sync closed #236 (2026-11-13). Its issue is #242 (`feature`, `area: website`, the first beta milestone, In progress on the board). SES v2 raw content moved to W4. W2 was split into W2a to W2d (see the W2 design note of [29](29-phase-4.md)). W2a (`SENDMOCK_VAR_*` as the lowest variable scope; WH-07.2 ticked) and W2b (`fake.*`, `lorem` and Postman-style `{{$random...}}` aliases in the binary's and the mock's engines and the template reference; WH-07.3 ticked) and W2d (`--templates-dir`: file templates loaded at startup and reloaded on change, read-only; WH-09.4) are built; W2c (multipart Fields bodies, WH-07.16) moved to W7. W2 is done: every WH-09 criterion is ticked and WH-09 accepted, and WH-07 waits only for WH-07.16. W3 was split into W3a to W3c (see the W3 design note of [29](29-phase-4.md)). W3a (real SendGrid ECDSA, Svix and Mailgun signatures in the templates, `{% set %}` and `unix` in the mock's engine, provider fixtures for the three) and the first part of W3b (`mailgun.events`, `postmark.webhooks` and `brevo.events` with their event name filters and fixtures; 28 templates) are built. Further W3b parts are built: `ses.sns` in SNS's documented envelope with the SES message inside, Twilio's `MessageStatus=read` for `sms.opened`, and the Email provider SDKs workflow verifying SendMock's SendGrid, Resend and Mailgun webhooks with `@sendgrid/eventwebhook`, the Resend SDK's `webhooks.verify` and Mailgun's documented HMAC (and the SendGrid SDK fix: its `setApiKey` resets the base URL). The rest of W3b added Africa's Talking, Sinch, Infobip, SparkPost and Google Chat (33 templates; Zapier and Make use the flat JSON template; Mandrill and MessageBird wait), and W3c ticked the proven criteria (EM-10, EM-11 and SM-10 accepted). W3 is done. W4 was split into W4a and W4b (see the W4 design note of [29](29-phase-4.md)). W4a (the provider operations shared by both areas: the nine email provider operations and custom provider creation for SMS and email; EM-18.5 and SM-16.5 ticked) and the first part of W4b (`[[email.providers]]` and custom providers in the seed file, Mail > Providers in the live UI suite; EM-18.7 ticked) are built. **Next:** the rest of W4b (SES v2 raw content, custom SMS providers in the live suite, EM-18.8 and SM-16.8 once the SDK workflows pass), then W5 (email quality checks). W5 is split into W5a to W5c (see its design note); W5a (the HTML compatibility check from caniemail data; EM-13.1 ticked) is built. PY-08.1 waits for the `subscription.*` events (Phase 4), PY-07.3 for answering disputes (Phase 4), and PY-09.3 for W11 (the Simulate menu live). Every commit through W10c is pushed (2026-11-05). Read CI for the newest commit and fix failures (watch the Rust coverage step and UI-08.3, which failed once each earlier, and the Stripe SDKs workflow, which first ran on `1849e1b`). **Pause after each finished step:** update this file, show the owner the resume prompt below, and wait before starting the next one. Read the workstream table and the design notes of [28](28-phase-3p.md) before starting.
- **Left open after W8:** no SM-16 criterion is ticked yet: SM-16.5 needs custom providers (Phase 4), SM-16.6 the CLI and MCP surfaces plus the skill's `add-sms-provider` workflow, SM-16.8 the SDK tests (W9). Adding a provider (`POST /api/v1/sms/providers`) comes with custom providers in Phase 4.
- **Progress at a glance (2026-11-05):** Stage A done (U0 to U3). Stage B: Phase 0 done, Phases 1 and 2 done apart from their beta releases, Phase 3 done apart from its beta release (23 workstreams, W1 to W11), Phase 3P done apart from its beta release (25 workstreams (W1, W2a, W3a, W3b, W2b, W4, W5a, W5b, W6, W7a, W7b, W8a, W8b, W8c, W9a, W9b, W10a, W10b, W10c, W11a, W11b, W11c, W12a, W12b and W12c; W7 and W9 were split in two, W8, W10, W11 and W12 in three), Phases 4 and 5 not started. 129 of 180 checklist items in this file; 30 of 108 features accepted, 290 of 497 criteria ticked, 408 of 497 with tests.
- **Before asking to commit:** `cargo fmt --all --check`, `bun run format:check`, `bun run lint` and `bun run typecheck` in `web/` when it changed, `bun run typecheck` in `website/` when it changed, `bun scripts/acceptance.ts`, and no em-dash in the diff. Clippy (50 lines per function, tests too) and every test run in CI only (owner rule, 2026-11-13). A new operation also needs a sample in `crates/sendmock-api/tests/openapi_contract.rs` and an updated CLI snapshot: push, then copy insta's new snapshot from the failed CI log (`gh run view <id> --log-failed`) into the `.snap` by hand; check its usage and flags against `docs/api/cli-reference.json`. Run `cargo clean` after each commit.
- **Known for next time:** a test that passes on macOS and stalls on Linux can be reproduced locally with the `rust:1.98` Docker image (mount the worktree read-only, use a named volume for `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, run one test); that is how the SYN cookie stall in the 500-session SMTP test was found. CI job logs need a GitHub token, so read results through the public runs, jobs and check-run annotations APIs.
- **Phase 0 under way on `feat/phase-0` (2026-10-29):** one branch for the whole phase, a commit per workstream, one pull request; tests run in its CI. W0 settled the ports (1025 / 8025) and the licence (MIT OR Apache-2.0) and deferred the trademark check to before 1.0.
- **Promotional video, PR #49 merged into `develop` on 2026-10-30** and merged into `feat/phase-0` the same day; the trailer is on YouTube (https://youtu.be/OHR7pVFGTl4) and the landing page plays it. Plan and every owner decision are in [23](23-promotional-video.md). One Remotion composition in `video/src/trailer/`: `SendMockTrailerMechanicalTyping` (about 2:01, 60 fps, rendered in 4K), with eight real app pages after the AI scene, the ways to connect in the platforms scene, every platform on the closing card and mechanical keyboard typing ([23](23-promotional-video.md#mechanical-typing-sendmocktrailermechanicaltyping-2026-10-29)); the other cuts were removed at the owner's request. State of the owner's review:
  - Transitions never crossfade (pushes, zoom-throughs, a match cut into the closing card); every scene has a slow camera push; headlines rise word by word.
  - No background music (owner direction). Soft synthesized UI sound effects play on cues each scene builds from its own animation timing (`<scene>Cues` exports, `parts/scene-sounds.tsx`); typing plays one key press per character. The owner asked for ear-soothing sounds and found the old high event bell disturbing, so keep every sound soft, low and varied.
  - The closing card shows `shibbirweb.github.io/sendmock`.
- **Working on the video:**
  1. Preview: `bunx remotion studio src/index.ts --port 3123` in `video/` (the owner reviews in Studio first).
  2. After changing a sound itself: `bun run sounds` (writes `public/sounds/*.wav`). Timing changes need no regeneration.
  3. After UI changes worth showing in the tour: run the web app in mock mode (`bun run dev` in `web/`), then `bun run app-pages` in `video/`.
  4. Render the YouTube cut in 4K at 60 fps: `bunx remotion render src/index.ts SendMockTrailerMechanicalTyping out/sendmock-trailer-4k60.mp4 --scale=2 --codec=h264 --crf=18` (`out/` is ignored). Timings are design frames at 30 a second, played at 60 ([23](23-promotional-video.md#frame-rate-and-render-2026-10-29)).
- **Phase 0 planned, Stage B audited (#47, merged):** [23-phase-0.md](23-phase-0.md) splits Phase 0 into W0 to W12 with branches, deliverables, tests, criteria and dependencies. [24-stage-b-coverage.md](24-stage-b-coverage.md) maps all 221 UI operations to the phase that builds them; the gaps it found are now in the Stage B phases below. Owner decisions from the audit: the binary embeds the live UI build only from Phase 1, with **Not available yet** for operations not built yet (new feature UI-12, `available_operations` in `GET /info`, [07](07-ui.md#not-available-yet-stage-b)); virtual numbers move to Phase 2 (SM-12); UI and API auth lands in Phase 1. Open point in [23](23-phase-0.md#criteria): whether the three-OS smoke job proves DS-01.2 for the host build, or it waits for the release binaries.

The sign-off pull request from `chore/u3-sign-off` ticks W11 and the sign-off, signs off every row of the review table in [18](18-phase-u3.md#review-table) (UI-07.3, with new rows for Mail providers, SMS providers and Scenarios), ticks 28 gate criteria (27 proven by passing tests, plus UI-07.3 proven by the sign-off) and sets UI-05 to UI-10, WS-02 and WS-04 to `UI done`. WS-02.4 (the video player) and WS-04.2 (snippets against a real instance) wait for Stage B.
- **Merged before it:** #40 (versioned docs, Install with AI), #42 (W18 update checker, F18), #43 (PR number fix) and #44 (the ask-first rule for commits, pushes and branches in [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md)). #45 was closed unmerged (an older copy of #44). #41 is the GitHub Discussions welcome post; Discussions share numbers with issues and pull requests.
- **Still open on GitHub (checked 2026-10-29):** merged PR #42 is still titled "Feat/update checker"; rename it to `feat:[U3] update checker with Off, Manual and Daily modes, release notes and the upgrade command for the running platform` so the first changelog lists it.

- **Working rules** (also in [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md)): ask before creating a branch, committing or pushing; tests and builds run in GitHub CI, never locally (only format, lint, typecheck, `cargo fmt` and `bun scripts/acceptance.ts` locally, owner rule of 2026-11-13; an out-of-date insta snapshot is fixed from the CI log); CI runs on pull requests and on pushes to `develop` and `main`, not on other branch pushes; check CI with `gh` (or the public GitHub API when it is not logged in); install and setup docs are numbered steps with each command in its own block; never use the em-dash character; no co-authors or AI tool names in commits or pull requests.
- **To pick up in a fresh session,** paste this prompt:

  ```text
  Read AGENTS.md, then docs/plan/PROGRESS.md (Next up, and the first "Resume here" bullet). Run no tests or builds locally (only format, lint, typecheck, cargo fmt, cargo check, actionlint and the acceptance script; cargo clean after); CI runs the rest. `1.3.1` is released and `main` is the default branch (always pass `--base develop` to `gh pr create`). After any roadmap change run `bun scripts/roadmap/build-feature-issues.ts`, `sync-issues.ts`, `update-roadmap-overview.ts` and `sync-project-board.ts`. 1.4.0 (A19, A23, A24) is merged into `develop` (#345) and being released: the first Resume here bullet says where the beta and stable stand. The owner said on 2026-11-22 to build all of 1.4.0, open its pull request, release the beta and then the stable without stopping to ask, and the same for every later version; stop only on a real failure or a product decision the plan does not settle. A macOS job GitHub cancels with no failed step, or a runner that loses contact, is a runner hiccup: `gh run rerun <run> --failed`; merge with `gh pr merge <n> --merge --author-email shibbirweb@gmail.com`. Put each PR's post-merge PROGRESS.md notes in its last commit. Chocolatey (#298, `Waiting on others`): once Chocolatey approves the queued versions, run Install channels with `channels=chocolatey` and close #298.
  ```

## How to update this file

- Feature completeness is tracked separately in [acceptance/](acceptance/README.md): a phase is only `Done` when the features it delivers are `Accepted` (or `UI done` for Stage A phases).
- An item is only ticked when its tests are merged and green in GitHub Actions (see Testing in [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md#testing)).
- Tick an item when its pull request is **merged into `develop`**, and add the PR number: `- [x] App shell (#12)`.
- Change a phase's status in the overview table when it starts or finishes, and move "Current position" along.
- Add a line to the [Update log](#update-log) for anything notable (phase done, scope change, release).
- Status values: `Done`, `In progress`, `Not started`, `Blocked`, `Dropped`.
- Scope changes go into the relevant plan doc first, then here.

## Decisions

### Made

| Date | Decision | Where |
|------|----------|-------|
| 2026-10-24 | Rust backend, single binary, SQLite; Bun + Vite + React + shadcn/ui frontend embedded | [02](02-architecture.md) |
| 2026-10-24 | UI first with mock data (MSW); backend starts only after UI sign-off | [11](11-roadmap.md) |
| 2026-10-24 | Every feature reachable from UI, REST, CLI, MCP and agent skill (one operation registry) | [14](14-ai-integration.md) |
| 2026-10-24 | Webhooks signed with Standard Webhooks, not a custom scheme | [05](05-webhooks.md#signing) |
| 2026-10-24 | SMS magic numbers match Twilio's official test numbers and error codes | [04](04-sms.md#delivery-simulation) |
| 2026-10-24 | Email accepted via SMTP and provider HTTP APIs; Twilio Verify emulated | [03](03-email.md), [04](04-sms.md) |
| 2026-10-24 | Docker is a first-class release channel | [09](09-distribution.md#docker) |
| 2026-10-24 | `develop` = beta, `main` = stable, releases cut by workflows (phpdebugger model) | [16](16-website-and-releases.md) |
| 2026-10-24 | Website on GitHub Pages with Fumadocs + Magic UI, API playground with in-browser demo mode | [16](16-website-and-releases.md) |
| 2026-10-25 | Add a fake payment gateway (Stripe, PayPal, SSLCommerz first; bKash, Razorpay, Paddle next) with test checkout and IPN; only test cards accepted | [17](17-payments.md) |
| 2026-10-25 | Publish from the `shibbirweb` GitHub account | [10](10-open-questions.md) |
| 2026-10-25 | Commits authored as `MD. Shibbir Ahmed <shibbirweb@gmail.com>` (rule in `AGENTS.md`); existing local commits rewritten before the first push | [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md#commits-and-pull-requests) |
| 2026-10-25 | Product name **SendMock** (binary and packages `sendmock`) | [10](10-open-questions.md#1-product-name-decided-2026-10-25-sendmock) |
| 2026-10-25 | SQLite is the only database: embedded (`rusqlite`, bundled, FTS5), one file, WAL mode, on disk at the OS data directory by default and `--db memory` for CI, hand-rolled `user_version` migrations | [02](02-architecture.md#database-sqlite) |
| 2026-10-29 | From Phase 1 the binary embeds the live UI build only; operations not built yet show Not available yet with their phase (UI-12) | [07](07-ui.md#not-available-yet-stage-b), [24](24-stage-b-coverage.md) |
| 2026-10-29 | STARTTLS is opt-in (`--smtp-starttls`, or implied by a certificate or `--smtp-require-tls`): the cross-language check found that Symfony Mailer (and so Laravel), Nodemailer, Rails and MailKit upgrade whenever STARTTLS is offered and then reject the self-signed certificate, so offering it by default broke their default settings; Mailpit and MailHog behave the same way. EM-02.1 is unchanged: STARTTLS works with the generated certificate once it is on | [03](03-email.md#options), [25](25-phase-1.md#w4-smtp-server) |
| 2026-10-29 | Virtual numbers and Messaging Services backend moved from Phase 4 to Phase 2 | [11](11-roadmap.md), [24](24-stage-b-coverage.md) |
| 2026-10-29 | UI and API auth (`--ui-auth`, API token) lands in Phase 1 with the first beta | [11](11-roadmap.md), [24](24-stage-b-coverage.md) |
| 2026-10-29 | Default ports SMTP `1025` and HTTP `8025` (Mailpit compatible) | [10](10-open-questions.md#2-default-ports-decided-2026-10-29-1025--8025) |
| 2026-10-29 | Licence `MIT OR Apache-2.0` (`LICENSE-MIT`, `LICENSE-APACHE`) | [10](10-open-questions.md#7-licence-decided-2026-10-29-mit-or-apache-20) |
| 2026-10-29 | Phase 0 on one branch, `feat/phase-0`, with a commit per workstream and one pull request | [23](23-phase-0.md#workstreams-and-order) |
| 2026-10-30 | Phase 3 W5 is split into W5a (catalogue and storage), W5b (templates on endpoints) and W5c (import and export, the template reference); template revisions and import/export are Phase 3, not Phase 4 | [27](27-phase-3.md#workstreams-and-order), [11](11-roadmap.md) |
| 2026-10-30 | Multipart Fields bodies (with file fields) move to Phase 4 as WH-07.16, next to the inbound email formats that send them; WH-07.6 covers JSON and form, the encodings the Stage A contract and request editor have | [12](12-webhook-variables.md), [acceptance](acceptance/03-webhooks.md#wh-07-variables-and-templating) |

### Open

See [10-open-questions.md](10-open-questions.md). Summary:

| Question | Recommendation | Status |
|----------|----------------|--------|
| Trademark check for "SendMock" | search USPTO, EUIPO, WIPO before branding | Deferred to before 1.0 (2026-10-29) |
| Provider emulation priority | Twilio, Vonage, then others; any other vendor is added as a custom provider, which replaced the Generic HTTP adapter ([22](22-configurable-providers.md)) | Open (using recommendation) |
| SDK redirection per SDK | verify in Phase 4 | Done: the Email provider SDKs, SMS clients, Payment apps and HTTP presets workflows and the recipe examples run each SDK against a release binary (2026-11-06) |
| Attachment storage | inside SQLite | Open (using recommendation) |
| Telemetry | none, opt-in update check | Open (using recommendation) |
| Mailpit API aliases | after 1.0 if demanded | Open |
| Persistence default | on-disk SQLite, `--db memory` for CI | Decided 2026-10-25 |
| caniemail data licence | confirm before shipping | Open |
| Lenient vs strict provider emulation | lenient default, strict mode per provider | Open (using recommendation) |
| Docs-site CORS default for local playground | allow docs origin, connect on click | Open |
| Fumadocs static export and search on Pages | static export with a static search index the browser downloads (`staticGET`), live on Pages since #1 | Decided 2026-10-25 |
| Magic UI MCP command for Claude Code | the Magic UI MCP server in the repo's `.mcp.json` ([16](16-website-and-releases.md)) | Decided 2026-10-25 |

| 2026-10-29 | CI fix: on Linux the 500-session SMTP test still stalled after the barrier fix. The runners keep 256 half-open connections (`tcp_max_syn_backlog`) and then use SYN cookies, where a lost final ACK leaves the client connected and the server unaware; SMTP waits for the server's greeting, so that session never starts and the barrier never opens. Reproduced in a Linux container; the test now keeps at most 64 connections in their handshake at once, and all 500 sessions are still open together. |

| 2026-10-29 | Promotional video started again from scratch at the owner's request (the earlier films were cleared). Research on developer-tool launch videos gave an eight-part structure in [23](23-promotional-video.md#film-structure-researched-2026-10-29-rewritten-after-a-directors-review): hook, problem, introduce SendMock, how it fits in, core workflow, the range, proof, call to action. The video now has its own plan file, [23](23-promotional-video.md). The audience now includes vibe coders (people building apps with an AI coding agent), since an agent can run SendMock fully through MCP. The video's length is not set yet; it is decided once the script is written. No voice-over; copyright-free generated music is added at the end, after the picture is approved. A director's review rewrote the eight parts as six acts with one throughline (Acme Shop, order #1042), a motif (a request goes out, the answer comes back), the AI agent as the climax and a bookended ending. A second review applied eight fixes: the motif is taught literally the first time, the cold open shows one concrete moment of pain, the turn only reveals, the proof shows the provider's own shape, the build is capped at three beats read by colour, the climax opens on stillness, the final card has one primary action, and people (Maya, the developer, the person asking the agent) are present through what they do. A third review made the payment the spine: the story follows a real shop's order of events (code, payment, then receipt and delivery text); the cold open's pain is an order stuck on "Payment pending"; the proof flips it to "Paid"; and Acme Shop gets a visible order page whose status says each outcome in plain words for both audiences. A fourth review fixed the turn's cause and effect (the one-line change comes before the first answer), taught the motif with the card charge alone, and put webhook templates in the climax (SendGrid's format from the built-in templates). The first scene plan, act 1 (the cold open), is written: five shots from the checkout to the stuck "Payment pending". Its review led to five fixes: it starts on the press, `localhost:3000` marks it as a local test, the payment service's answer is shown turned back at the edge of the laptop's light (the real problem), two refreshes add a human beat, and the hold is kept short. After research on cold opens, showing invisible technical problems and silent-film reading speed, the laptop became a visible object whose edge stops the answer, labels appear only once still, and every line holds about one second per word plus one. Owner review of the first build: it read as a live shop failing, so act 1 now opens in the developer's code editor and shows their whole setup (editor, terminal, the app at `localhost:3000`), shot as a cinematic trailer. Owner direction: email and SMS are SendMock's main focus, so the story now follows Maya's verification email, sign-in code and payment (payment third), act 1 shows all three channels stuck with one message ("Your app talks. Nobody talks back."), and the look follows researched cinematic-shot rules (tilted planes, a thin focus band, hard cuts on the action, no visible cursor, a dark stage with a pool of light). Owner direction: the email and SMS beats are messages that expect a reply through the provider (an email question answered by email reply, posted back by the provider's inbound route; a "Reply YES" text answered by text, posted back by the provider's inbound webhook), so all three beats share one shape: the app sends, the reply comes back through a webhook, and locally it never arrives. Owner review: the cuts from code to page did not match, so every cut now keeps the eye trace (each page's sending control opens where the typed line ended), the drift keeps one direction, and the typed statement highlights as it is sent. Owner direction: act 1 is now simple, in big words like the reference launch film, describing the problem with email, SMS, payments and any third-party API whose answers (replies, receipts, webhooks) cannot reach localhost, in ten typographic beats ending on "Your app talks. Nobody talks back." The film's look is now a dark theme chosen by the owner: GitHub's dark background (`#0d1117`) with soft drifting teal and emerald glows, off-white words and a calm mint accent, tuned to be easy on the eyes. Owner review: the act 1 experiments did not look professional, so a feature trailer is made from scratch in the Screen Studio style (the real SendMock UI captured at each state of a flow, a floating window, smooth zooms onto the action), one chapter per feature: email, SMS, payments, webhooks, the HTTP catcher. |

| 2026-11-07 | Fix: on Windows runners the retention load test twice saw one SMTP message wait 8 to 9 s (PL-08.2). The prune's `wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` copied the whole write-ahead log and synced it inside the writer's queue, holding every ingest write behind a slow disk. A passive checkpoint on its own connection now does the copy beside ingest first, so the truncate in the queue has almost nothing left; vacuum steps are 250 pages. A store test proves a checkpoint finishes while the writer is held. That alone did not end it: the next Windows run stalled 8.0 s again, with the test running beside about a thousand others. The load test now runs alone (`threads-required` in `.config/nextest.toml`), so it measures SendMock rather than a busy runner. |

| 2026-11-08 | Fix: CI on 57b9ab1 failed WH-07.10 twice in one end-to-end shard: after `fill("")` on the template body the editor still held the old body minus its first `{`, with the typed text in front. Playwright's `fill` selects through the DOM, which CodeMirror reads back asynchronously, so its delete reached CodeMirror with the cursor at the start and removed one character. Every end-to-end test that replaces a CodeMirror editor's text (7 places in 4 specs) now uses `replaceCodeEditorText`, which selects all through CodeMirror's own keymap, types the new text and checks it. |

Every other workflow passed except one end-to-end shard (the CodeMirror race fixed in the next commit). |
| 2026-11-08 | Issue #171, first samples (Retention on Windows on d5bb92e): 2 of 18 runs stalled, one with the Defender exclusion and one without, and real-time protection is off on the runners, so Defender is ruled out. The failing run's warnings found the cause: a write ran 4132 ms with no queue wait, the SMTP message behind it waited 3912 ms in the queue (its body step took 4.1 s), and a checkpoint pass took 7769 ms. The blocking write was the prune's `wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` in the writer's queue, which copies and syncs whatever was written since the passive copy; the background thread's own truncate did the same under SQLite's write lock about every second during a burst, since a passive pass reports the whole log copied even under steady writes. Fix: pruning ends with a passive copy only, and the background thread cuts the log only once writes have paused (no growth since the last pass) or past 256 MiB; the slow checkpoint warning now splits `passive_ms` and `truncate_ms` ([02](02-architecture.md#concurrency-and-performance-notes)). The sampling workflow runs again on this change. |
| 2026-11-08 | Issue #171, after the fix (Retention on Windows on 6150ef7): 0 of 18 runs stalled, with no slow write or checkpoint warning, against 2 of 18 before. A clean 18 could still be chance (about 12% at the old rate), so the sampling workflow now runs the test 30 more times in 6 jobs without the Defender split, which the first samples ruled out; with 48 clean runs the test is turned back on for Windows and the issue closed. |
| 2026-11-08 | Issue #171, 30 more samples (2068101): 2 of 30 stalled, with two causes in the new split timings. One (2.96 s) was the background truncate itself, 2876 ms with almost nothing to copy (`passive_ms` 57), meeting the first message of the test's second round after a short pause; the log is now cut only after five quiet passes instead of one. The other (6.26 s) involved no truncate and no lock: the message's own write ran 6.2 s while passive copies took 10.6 s and 13.3 s, which points at the runner's disk rather than SendMock. Sampling runs again on the change. |

| 2026-11-12 | Owner report: when an app sends thousands of emails, the web UI stops showing new mail. Cause: every realtime event calls `invalidateQueries` on `["emails"]`, which cancels the list request in flight and starts another, so none finishes; each event also shows a toast, and the server skips events for a lagging WebSocket client silently. Plan: W19 in [30](30-phase-5.md), designed after reading how Mailpit does it (list inserts from the event's data, a `stats` event at most 4 times a second, a 2 second notification pause, slow clients dropped); new criteria PL-02.3 to PL-02.5, so PL-02 is back to In progress ([#224](https://github.com/shibbirweb/sendmock/issues/224)). |

| 2026-11-12 | W19 built (#225): the web UI applies realtime events in 250 ms batches, writes new emails straight into the cached email lists (no list request), refreshes other queries at most once per batch without cancelling a request in flight, puts a batch in the bell with one update, shows at most one toast or browser notification every 2 seconds ("and N more"), and refreshes every list once after a reconnect; the server closes a WebSocket client that falls behind (close code 4000, `lagged`) and ends its SSE stream.
